# moss-multi progress

**Overall: 96% done** (301 of 311 planned tasks verified)

| Milestone | What a person can newly do | Tasks verified | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| M0 Foundation | Open the real moss shell from the built Worker; sign up and in; a note survives a restart | 19 / 19 | in progress |
| M1 Two people, one note | Share a note and co-edit live with presence, cursors, shared titles | 20 / 20 | in progress |
| M2 Workspace and access | Folders, trash, full sharing, live revocation, stranger on a phone | 13 / 13 | in progress |
| M3 Rich workspace | Media, HTML/embeds, every node family, search, vaults, agent keys, read-only viewer | 78 / 75 | in progress |
| M4 Comments | Moss's full comment experience as CRDT data | 21 / 21 | in progress |
| M5 Suggestions | Suggest mode, vetting, accept/reject | 33 / 32 | in progress |
| M6 History | Versions, view, diff, identity-preserving restore | 21 / 21 | in progress |
| M7 Agents and local sync | CLI pull/push/sync, Bot presence, folder-watch daemon | 27 / 27 | in progress |
| M8 Ship | Everything on a permanent staging URL with demo content | 19 / 20 | |
| M9 Polish | Understand every control without help: fixes found by using the whole product | 50 / 45 | |

A task counts only after an independent checker passes it on green CI. Each milestone also ends with the cumulative journey suite green in Chromium and WebKit and a naive-user critic pass.

## Log

- 2026-10-08 — T3.S7 verified: an oversized JSON body sent to any API or sign-in route now gets 413 before the server buffers it (64 KiB cap; feedback and note creation have their own caps), so a huge request can no longer exhaust a Worker's memory.
- 2026-10-08 — T3.13 verified: closing or removing an editor with an open chart or HTML draft now saves that draft before going read-only, typing right after inserting a chart from the slash menu (or composing with an IME after a selected block) lands after it in order, and CI holds the editor's static critical path to 650 KB gzip.
- 2026-10-08 — T3.S6 verified: a large markdown paste (40,000 paragraphs, a 30,000-item list, a 5,000-row table, megabytes of text) now lands whole in one undo step for the pasting person and every collaborator, or is refused whole with nothing applied when it would pass the note's size cap.
- 2026-10-08 — T3.S4 verified: a note full of unclosed brackets, huge lines or wide tables now imports, pastes and exports in time linear in its size (2 MB in under 5 s), with ordinary notes byte-identical to moss and over-budget lines kept as literal text.
- 2026-10-08 — T3.S6b verified: a 30,000-item list pasted mid-note now reaches a collaborator and a fresh joiner whole, without the tab stalling or the socket reopening.
- 2026-10-08 — T3.F verified: the media-revalidation tests now hold copies and duplicates at a fixed point and pass reliably, so a flaky run no longer blocks a PR.
- 2026-10-08 — T3.F2 verified: typing a slash command and pressing Enter now always inserts what the typed query names, so `/media` inserts media even on a busy page instead of a row from the previous list.
- 2026-10-08 — T4.R2 verified: comments now sit on the final M3, so a long comment or reply and a note created with a full comments sidecar fit under the request caps, and a large paste in a commented note lays out remote cursors once per batch instead of stalling.
- 2026-10-09 — T5.R2 verified: suggestions now sit on the final M4, and a large paste in Suggest mode lands whole as one suggestion within every suggestion cap or is refused whole with the pasted blocks offered back, never half-applied.
- 2026-10-09 — T5.S3 verified: Backspace or Delete in Suggest mode past a long struck run (20,000 characters) now answers quickly, reading each node once while still deleting whole graphemes and keeping own-versus-body routing.
- 2026-10-07 — T6.1 verified: a note body and its payload docs can be reconciled to a target version in place, keeping the Yjs identity of untouched blocks so a peer's concurrent insert and comment anchors survive, and a result that would not export the target is refused.
- 2026-10-07 — T6.2 verified: each note now keeps automatic versions (on last disconnect and on activity, deduplicated, large ones spilled to R2) and rate-limited named versions over REST, its history bounded by pruning rather than charging anyone, and a restore whose result fails verification is refused with 409.
- 2026-10-07 — T6.3 verified: a person can open a note's History, browse automatic and named versions, save a named checkpoint, view any version or diff it against the current note, and restore it in place with a confirmation.
- 2026-10-07 — T6.S2 verified: named versions saved through an agent key now count against the agent's owner for the per-person cap and the save rate, so an editor can no longer bypass their own limits with an agent key.
- 2026-10-07 — T7.1 verified: a person or agent can use the `moss-multi` CLI to sign in by device login or API key, list notes, `cat`, `pull` and `add` notes byte-exact by id, URL or title prefix, read history and snapshots, comment, and share, with JSON output and defined exit codes.
- 2026-10-07 — T6.S3 verified: restoring a version whose code, HTML or formula payload changed at both ends now keeps the unchanged middle's identity, so a peer's concurrent insert there survives the restore.
- 2026-10-07 — T6.S1 verified: restoring a version now reconciles three-way from the base the restorer opened Restore on, so words a peer or agent typed after that base survive the restore, and a stale base or a dropped block someone typed into is refused with 409.
- 2026-10-08 — T6.S4 verified: restoring a version on a large note now succeeds while a peer types, because its restore point is staged in DocDO SQLite in the restore's turn and moved to R2 afterwards, and a crash before the move recovers on wake.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.1s verified: the CLI now keeps every local read and write inside the workspace (real paths, no links out), opens device sign-in only on the server's own origin, follows no redirects, never prints the key, and escapes server text before it reaches the terminal.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.2 verified: an agent can `push` an edited local note and it lands through a three-way structural merge against the base it pulled, so concurrent human edits survive and a push that would delete most of the note is refused unless forced.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.3 verified: an agent's push shows a Bot-badged presence chip, `push --suggest` lands as a pending suggestion, an agent granted commenter by its owner can pull and comment but is refused on push and disconnected on revoke, a revoked key closes the agent's socket and 401s the CLI, and `comments` and `suggestions` list a note's threads and open suggestions.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.4 verified: `moss-multi sync` keeps a local folder in step with the web: local edits appear on the web, web edits update the file, untracked files become docs titled from their stem, local deletes stay local, and moss vault notes keep their `# Title` line and comment markers.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.L verified: CI's lint now fails any regex that can backtrack super-linearly, and every flagged one, including the CLI title-line parse and moss's note-type classifier, is a linear scan with the same results, so a hostile note or title can no longer stall the editor or server on a regex.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.S1 verified: pushing a note with `%%m:` comment markers, images or trailing whitespace now converts in linear-time scans with the same output as moss, so a hostile 2 MB push can no longer stall the server.
- 2026-10-08 — T7.S2 verified: a push merge now runs on one counted step budget instead of a clock, so a costly or hostile push can no longer stall the server; a drifted push past the budget is refused 409 `push-unverified` with nothing landed, and a degenerate push is refused before the doc's own edits are diffed.

- 2026-10-08 — T7.S4 verified: `moss-multi sync` adopting a moss note now records the created revision as its base right after create, so a peer edit made before the first read survives a later local edit, and a retry after a failed create resumes the tracked doc instead of making a second one.

- 2026-10-09 — T8.3 verified: the security sweep now runs against staging and passes all 52 checks (headers, header stripping, existence leaks, origin gate, revocation, SSRF, body caps through the edge, rate limits including spoofed client IPs), with the staging column recorded in docs/SECURITY.md.

- 2026-10-09 — T6.R verified: history now sits on the final M5, and restoring a version of a note with thousands of payloads is accepted instead of refused 413, because the restore base has its own cap sized from the state cap and its write token is charged before the body is read.
- 2026-10-10 — T5.S8 verified: the Suggestions panel fetches previews only for cards on screen, expanded or active (two at a time), reuses them when reopened on an unchanged note, and waits out a 429's Retry-After before asking again.
- 2026-10-10 — T5.S12 verified: every refused suggestion frame now counts toward the refusal cooldown, a connection refused for lack of room gets cheap refusals for its growth frames for a while, and suggestion leases pay the write rate, so a suggester can no longer spin the server with refused frames.

- 2026-10-12 — T8.B1 verified: the security sweep's body caps now count only the Worker's own JSON `too-large` 413, its revocation checks revoke only a socket the server has admitted and require a 4403 or 4402 close, and the authz matrix checks every `/api` route by method as well as path, with the exact refusal status and JSON error.

- 2026-10-18 — T7.P1 verified: a `.moss-multi/scope.json` (allow, follow with depth, deny always winning) now limits sync, pull, push and add to in-scope notes, so an agent on a host only uploads allowlisted notes, notes reached by links stay download only until `scope confirm` adds them, and a folder without a scope file syncs as before.

- 2026-11-23 — T5.S16 built: before it, a person with one note open in two Suggest windows could lose everything typed while offline in one of them, with no warning, when the other window reconnected first; and an ordinary second window or a reconnect could lock them out of the note for a minute with a notice about the connection limit that nothing fixed. Now a reconnect is never refused for the person's own reservations, so the offline text lands; reconnecting, a second or third window and a stalled connection's late frames draw no refusal, so there is no lockout; if a cooldown still happens (forged frames), the note says suggesting is paused and the text is kept, and the window reconnects by itself and delivers it, even when the refused frames arrived on the window's own connection; and text offered back after a closed suggestion makes the browser ask before a reload, and a sign-out or in-app leave ask or stay. Still unverified: whether the nine closes seen on staging had this cause, and what happens on a real network (whether the server sees a dropped socket close, and whether a stalled connection's frames arrive after its replacement).

### T9.55e — a replayed GET waits until the closing connections have closed

- Cause: `selftest/stack.spec.ts:53` ("every request handed to the stack as its pooled connections idle out is answered at once") failed `GET after 4976 ms idle: no answer in 3 s` in 6 of 103 WebKit and 1 of 103 Chromium shards of the three full lanes with slow legs. The GET waited in `wrangler dev`'s proxy and was released about 8 s later by the next burst: wrangler's `requestRetryQueue`. The Worker closes the pooled connections of one burst together 5 s after their last use; the patch replayed a lost GET at once onto the next pooled connection, which was closing too, and after four lost attempts the GET was parked until the next request.
- Fix: `patches/wrangler@4.113.0.patch` makes a replay wait the way a POST already does: while the newest pooled connection is 4.5 s old or more, it waits until that connection is past its idle close. The pool hands out the newest first, so a loss on it means every other pooled connection is closing as well, and one replay is then enough. The three replays, the selftest and its 3 s bound are unchanged. `scripts/wrangler-proxy.test.mjs` has the case (six connections idling out together, one GET lost, answered on one replay, nothing parked).
- Aimed measurement (one GET landing 4,988 to 4,994 ms after six connections were used together, 282 GETs per rule in both engines): with the replay sent at once, 62 first attempts lost and 18 GETs unanswered in 3 s; with the wait, 76 lost and none unanswered, each answered on the first replay after a wait of 300 to 306 ms.
- Cost: a GET whose first forward is lost is answered about 0.3 s later (at most 0.8 s by the patch's constants). A lost WebSocket upgrade is a GET and waits the same. In a lane on the fix about nine selftest GETs per shard took 0.3 s, so "answered at once" in the selftest's title includes them.
- What is left: the wait fires only when the proxy's model of its pool says the newest connection is 4.5 s old or more, so a model that disagrees with the real pool replays at once as before; a GET that still loses four attempts is still parked.

### T9.55f — a refused screenshot is a recorded skip

- Base rate: Chromium 149 refused the first evidence capture of the recording selftest's control (`page.screenshot: Protocol error (Page.captureScreenshot): Unable to capture screenshot`) in 23 of about 8,041 executions in the twelve days to 2026-11-25 (1 in 350), and 26 of about 10,044 since the selftest arrived. Each refusal failed the selftest and so stopped its shard's journeys: 288 legs did not run.
- Cause: `Actors.checkpoint` let a refused `@evidence` capture throw in place of the findings; only the capture in `Actors.fail` had a guard (since T3.B27). Chromium answers no within a few milliseconds when the image it was handed is empty; that the page had not yet put its first frame on a surface is inferred from one live refusal. Why the control's place is about 50 times likelier than the same steps elsewhere is not known. The untested lead: a capture and a context close of the same page just before the control. With `screenshot: 'only-on-failure'` Playwright itself captures every page at each `context.close()`, pass or fail, and swallows a refusal there, so no log can show a refusal at that point.
- Fix: both captures go through one helper; a refused one is skipped and recorded on the test as a `capture-skipped` annotation naming the attachment and the first line of the error. The new selftest in `e2e/selftest/lib.spec.ts` pins both guards. The reporters look annotations up by type, the staging summary drops types it does not name, and off loopback nothing is captured.
- What to watch: once the recording selftest's control stops asking Chromium for pixels (the `m8` part of this section), no selftest sends a real capture through `Actors`, so a refusal in a journey shows only as a `capture-skipped` annotation on that test; their count is the number to watch.

### T9.55h — the macos job runs the `@macos` selftests and legs; `duplicated` stops depending on the pace of typing

- Wiring: the `macos` job ran `--project=selftest-webkit --project=webkit --grep @macos`. Playwright never applies `--grep` to a dependency project, so since the first `@macos` journey (2026-10-03) every selftest ran there first, the `@slow` ones too (the step sets no `SLOW`). The `@macos` leg (`j02-title.spec.ts:606`) ran in 2 of the 13 jobs that planned it since then; in the others a selftest failed first.
- The disk selftest (`selftest/stack.spec.ts:78`, "the stack keeps answering writes while the runner disk flushes a backlog") failed 8 of 8 on macOS: the runner's flush is too short for ten spaced writes, or the writes are too slow. It passes on the Linux shards, whose stack state is on tmpfs.
- `duplicated` (`selftest/detectors.spec.ts`) failed 7 of 13 on macOS: its fixture echoed the typed text 200 ms after the last input, and macOS WebKit stalled 0.7 to 1.0 s between two keys in 12 of 20 typings, so the echo held a partial string. Forced with a 300 ms pause after five characters, the old fixture failed 6 of 6 in Linux Chromium, Linux WebKit and macOS WebKit. A test bug on every engine. The fixture now echoes only when the selftest calls `window.echo()` after typing, and a second case types with that pause.
- The job now runs the `@macos` selftests (the two Backspace proofs), then the `@macos` legs with `--no-deps`. A failed first step skips the second. What it no longer observes: nothing in CI types in macOS WebKit any more, so the keystroke stall is not seen anywhere; and its stack still keeps its storage on the runner's disk with nothing asking whether a write stalls behind a flush.
- The macOS stack and a flush, one probe on one runner: with its storage on the runner's disk a note creation took 84 to 1,896 ms by the stack's log during a flush (11 of 22 over 1 s; 4 of 22 over 2 s on the test's clock), and 3,774 ms once in another job; with `--state-dir` on a RAM disk, 14 to 284 ms (0 of 51 over 2 s). Every write answered 201 and was read back.
- The `m1` gate ended red and `m1` is in `main`: pull request 2 was merged three seconds after its gate run 37422837429 was created, and that run's `macos` job then failed.
- Two other selftests failed on macOS only while the whole project ran there: `rebind-keeps-text` (on `m8` and up; the same 200 ms timer, fixed on `m8`), and `lib.spec.ts:156` "the clock starts after the click returns" (1 of 15).

### T9.55b — a note's updated time is the time of its last edit

- Cause: `Projections.touch()` writes `docs.updated_at` at a note's first edit, then at most every 5 s; its trailing write read the clock when its timer fired, so it stamped "first edit plus 5 s" whatever the last edit's time. With two notes written back to back, the first one's trailing stamp was later than the second's edits: about 5 s after its first keystroke it jumped above the second in the newest-first list, and the second's own trailing stamp put it back 1 to 3 s later. A click on a row at the instant of a jump opened nothing; that is how the wiki-links leg of `j12-search` failed in WebKit (3 of 139 full-group runs).
- Experiment, the row swap is what loses the open: the target's trailing refetch held, a press on the source's row, the refetch let through, the release. Unchanged: Chromium 5 held, the rows changed places under the press each time and the source never opened; WebKit 4 held, the same 4 of 4, and 1 execution whose held refetch never answered after it was let through (counted apart). With the fix: Chromium 5 held, the rows stayed and the source opened 5 of 5; WebKit 3 held, the same 3 of 3, and 2 executions whose refetch never answered (counted apart). Runs 45675574803 and 45675579393.
- Fix: the trailing write carries the time of the last edit. The writes still happen at once and then at most every 5 s, and the D1 write is still `MAX(updated_at, ?)`, so a stamp never goes backwards. A note's "updated" time, and the order of the notes list, search results and the listing by it, is now its last edit, up to 5 s earlier than before.
- Readers of `docs.updated_at`, checked before the change: the notes list (`api/workspace.ts`, the bridge, Moss's list), search (`api/search.ts`), a doc's read and backlinks (`api/docs.ts`), folders and Trash listings, and Moss's per-folder newest note all sort or show it. None compares it with a clock, keeps a "newest seen" mark or asks for notes changed since a time. The command-line tool's `sync` and `watch` (on `m9`) hold it only as a field and do not use it to choose what to read. SearchDO, suggestions and comments keep their own times.
- Tests: `projections.test.ts` checks that the trailing touch stamps the twelfth edit and that no stamp is later than its write; a `j12-search` leg reads the list every 40 ms for 6.5 s after two notes are written and expects one order, the second note first. The split-navigation leg of `j00-persist` now expects C, B, A: B's trailing stamp is before C was created, so the list no longer moves after B's typing.
- What is left: a row can still move at a 5-second mark for a good reason (note A edited from 0 to 4 s and note B at 2 to 3 s leaves B on top until A's trailing write), and a click that straddles any row move still opens nothing, or the neighbouring note. `ui.openNote` was left as it is on purpose: a second click in a helper that most journeys use would hide a first click that opens nothing.

### T9.52b — a click beside a block, and the comment shortcut leg

- A click beside a block (a code block's line numbers, a block's frame) does not keep it selected, as in Moss at the pin: the block's click handler selects it and `@lexical/rich-text` clears that selection inside the same click. `e2e/parity/block-click.spec.ts` holds that behavior.
- No fix was kept, because claiming those clicks also keeps a block selected through clicks inside its own editing surface, and then a whole block is removed where nothing was before:
  - a stroke drawn on a selected canvas, then Backspace or Delete, removes the canvas;
  - a chart clicked, Edit, text in its JSON selected and cut, removes the chart;
  - on macOS, a click inside a code block's open text, then Ctrl+H or Ctrl+D, removes the code block.
- The j15 comment shortcut leg now selects the code block from the keyboard: the caret at the end of the paragraph above, ArrowDown, the selection outline, then the shortcut.

## T1.1s identity audit

- Members: signed-in link-only readers reached doc member names through either a doc or ancestor-folder token. Authorization now omits the token; folder members already used grants only. The regression run [37133412126](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/37133412126) failed all four doc cases (query/header × doc/folder link) with 200 instead of 404. Owner emails, grantee names, anonymous denial and owner-only writes remain covered.
- T1.5 closes the awareness-identity follow-up: ownership/grant-based recipients are enforced on initial snapshots, live frames and departure frames, including signed-in link-only readers. Worker and real DocDO harness regressions cover the boundary.
- Other reads: `api/docs.ts` returns document fields and role, no owner/creator identity; `api/workspace.ts` returns only the caller's own vault and docs; `/api/me` returns the caller alone. Comment-author and notification endpoints are not implemented (`api/router.ts`, `principal-do.ts` skeleton). T4.1/T4.3 must enforce identity privacy in synced comment data as well as REST, and T2.8 must recheck live grants on notification reads (A§8).

### T9.48b — the check-list paste stall

- Cause, by experiment (each a `repeat_each=5` Chromium run of j01-paste-check against the capture build, [control run 45018468321](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018468321): longest hold 2930-4648 ms, total 5331-6301 ms). Removing the app's `::highlight()` rules took it to 815-1319 ms and 2542-2869 ms ([run 45018478211](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018478211)). Comment paint skipping notes without comments ([45018483348](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018483348), 2959-3007 ms), keeping the gap paragraph ([45018493106](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018493106), 2234-2592 ms), leaving `dir` lifted ([45018488218](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018488218), 1803-2456 ms) and no second session ([45018497982](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018497982), 3113-4904 ms) did not separate. Unchanged m9 alongside: 3165-3588 ms ([45018473274](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45018473274)).
- Mechanism: the last batch and the `dir` restore after it each restyle the whole list (a style recalc, not layout: 1933 and 1894 ms with the rules, 409 and 333 ms without; [run 45019741565](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45019741565) against [45019750041](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45019750041)). M4 added 9 comment highlight names to moss's 5 (and M5 2 suggestion names), all unscoped, and Chromium resolves every unscoped highlight name on every element during a restyle. In a 10,000-item list the restyle took 17 ms with no rules, 35 ms with moss's 5, 64 ms with all 16, and 40 ms with the app's rules on the editor root (local Chromium bench). Those two whole-list restyles are still there at about 0.4 s each.
- Engine finding (Chromium 149): an unscoped `::highlight(name)` rule costs a highlight style resolution per element per name on every restyle, even with no highlight registered under that name. A rule on an ancestor reaches the text inside through highlight inheritance. WebKit has no highlight inheritance: a rule on the editor root paints nothing on text in a nested element, and `getComputedStyle(child, '::highlight(name)')` tells the two apart (it returns the ancestor's style in Chromium and the initial value in WebKit), so the app adds the per-element rules only where the probe says so. Adding per-element rules alongside the root's, even gated off, cost Chromium 25% more, and turning them on in one subtree was 20 times slower.
- WebKit: same-runner pairs of the paste with the per-element rules and with the old universal rules held the tab 884-1135 against 875-978 ms at a time and 5656-6270 against 5213-5341 ms in all (calibrations 46-78 ms), no difference beyond runner noise.
- Other paste journeys, longest hold in ms before ([run 45015179081](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45015179081)) and after ([run 45024592085](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45024592085)), Chromium then WebKit: paste-blocks 12,000 paragraphs 657→859, 1215→1488; 23,500 paragraphs 1660→1367, 1327→1594; paste-list 11,000 items 444→634, 633→577; nested 9,500 items 556→733, 645→499; paste-table 4,500 rows 545→506, 485→469; j01-paste 1,019,000 characters 1242→1023, 1677→1009; 200k between paragraphs 369→314, 764→805; paste-cap 44,000 paragraphs with code and HTML 3664→4472, 4277→4100; paste-check 4058→968, 577→958. The single runs move with the runner; only paste-check moved beyond that. `MAX_STALL_MS` stays at 5 s on CI: paste-cap's refused 44,000-paragraph paste holds 3.7-4.5 s in both engines.
- The collaborator's tab, now timed: it held 5.3-5.4 s at a time and 16 s in all while applying the paste before, 1.1-1.8 s and 4.1-5.2 s after; each hold is a doc socket message whose time is almost all forced style and layout.

### T9.48g — WebKit's network process

- Labelled, not retried: an invariant failure whose page logged "Network process crashed" is now BLOCKED with its census attached; "WebKit encountered an internal error" alone and "Target crashed" stay FAILED. Every failed WebKit test attaches `engine.json` (step 2 of the brief) before its contexts close, then their close times as `engine-closes.json`; both fixtures decide this in one teardown, `Actors.settle`. Selftests red in [run 45040888159](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45040888159), green in [run 45042426650](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45042426650). The forced-failure selftest goes through that teardown (an invariant failure on a live page) and was red while it did not capture ([run 45058033806](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45058033806)); its leg for a failed test body, with no invariant finding, was red in [run 45070740303](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45070740303).
- Scan: every WebKit e2e job of `ci.yml` since 2026-10-01 (all attempts, to 2026-11-22 17:05 UTC), read from its failure annotations; runner images from the job logs of the hits. A fault whose test failed without the text in its error (a silent hang, a timeout) is not counted.

| Jobs started (UTC) | WebKit e2e jobs | Failed | "Network process crashed" | "Target crashed" | Runner images of the hits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Oct 1-15 | 19,846 | 766 | 20 | 7 | ubuntu-24.04 20260927.320.1, 20261004.327.1, 20261012.334.1 |
| Oct 16-31 | 5,842 | 142 | 1 | 0 | ubuntu-24.04 20261012.334.1 |
| Nov 1-10 | 0 | | | | no WebKit job ran |
| Nov 11-15 | 2,622 | 44 | 8 | 1 | ubuntu-24.04 20261101.360.1, 20261111.127.1, 20261111.370.1 |
| Nov 16-20 | 4,435 | 85 | 6 | 3 | ubuntu-26.04 20261111.196.1 |
| Nov 21 | 1,638 | 37 | 3 | 3 | ubuntu-26.04 20261116.202.1, 20261116.88.1 |
| Nov 22 | 2,881 | 67 | 1 | 1 | ubuntu-26.04 20261116.202.1 |

- The rate is about 1 network-process crash per 950 WebKit jobs (39 in 37,264) and has been there since Oct 5 on every runner image, both Ubuntu releases: its onset does not match image 20261116.
- 33 of the 39 are in three journeys: j01-registers (14), j00-import G1 (10) and j11-embeds `:120` (9); then j01-editing (2) and one each in j01-presence, j01-paste-list, j03-connection and j11-editor-geometry. 13 of the 15 "Target crashed" (a page process) are in `export.spec.ts`, a separate recurring fault.
- After T9.48d (merged 2026-11-22 14:11 UTC) the scan saw 562 WebKit jobs and no crash. At the rate above that is what chance gives too, so it says nothing yet.
- Reproduction (`e2e/selftest/engine-repro.spec.ts`, `-f grep=@repro -f browsers=webkit -f repeat_each=10`): a same-origin JSON page on the stack, no product code, that aborts backlinks reads as they answer, cancels frame loads and opens the workspace and doc sockets, in 20 or 40 fresh contexts per repeat. Plain: no fault in 30 repeats and 1,000 contexts ([45043993736](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45043993736), [45044775980](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45044775980), [45044785400](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45044785400)). With the harness's interception (`context.route('**/*')` continuing every request, and the sever's WebSocket proxy): one silent hang in 20 repeats and 800 contexts (run 45044775980, repeat 9, context 9 of 40, 55 s and 49 contexts into the browser).
- At that hang the page's main thread answered in 58 ms, a fresh page in the same context got no answer in 5 s, a fresh context loaded `/api/version` in 460 ms and Node in 12 ms. The network process (15 s old, 26 descriptors, 67 MB resident, limit 65,536) wrote `GLib-CRITICAL g_atomic_ref_count_dec: assertion 'old_value > 0' failed`, then `libsoup-CRITICAL soup_session_feature_request_queued: assertion 'SOUP_IS_SESSION_FEATURE (feature)' failed` with `g_object_ref: assertion 'G_IS_OBJECT (object)' failed` for each later request. Memory, descriptors and `/dev/shm` (2% used) were not short.
- Engine finding (Playwright 1.61 WebKit, WPE build 2311, Linux): a reference count in the network process's libsoup session drops below zero, and every request queued on that session afterwards meets a freed object and never completes. It happened with no product code on the page and only with Playwright's interception on (also seen with no interception, on a product page: job 138317559276, T9.55c). That this is also the crash form (the freed object touched instead of skipped) is inferred, not shown, and Safari was not tried. j00-import G1 routes every page request and j01-registers severs Ben in several tests; j11-embeds `:120` routes nothing itself (later tests in its file do).
- j07's sign-in leg now checks the session cookie carries an expiry, which the claim that a normal window recovers from a network-process restart rests on; it does in both engines. The restart itself was not exercised.

### T9.48a — what the pin leaves for the owner (deviation 38)

- A window with nothing chosen still opens the first note that arrives, once: an empty vault, a switch in from an empty Home, or the last note trashed, then a collaborator's create.
- The pinned note becomes the note restored on reload (it is written as last-viewed, as a clicked note is).
- Not changed by the pin: j12-search's wiki-link test (run 44931221611, attempt 1) failed opening the target from its sidebar row while another note's socket closed and reopened; both notes there were chosen through "+ Note", so it is not this switching. It reads like the row reorder under a click that j00-persist describes; not investigated further.
- No other journey expects a note created over the API after its shell opened to come on screen unopened: the rest create before the shell loads (j01-autolink) or open the note by address or row.

### T9.48c — who runs moss's comment-marker regexes

Moss's marker patterns (`\{%c:\s*([A-Za-z0-9_,\-\s]+?)\s*%\}` and the `%%m:` forms) are cubic in a run of blanks after an opener. `common/comment-markers.ts` and `common/markdown-layers.ts` now find markers with the linear `commentMarkerAt` (recorded patches), held to moss's regexes by `note-store.linear.test.ts`. Each user of those sources:

- Save path, `packages/editor/src/desktop/pipeline.ts` (`migrateLegacyCommentMarkersToModern`) and `note-store.port.ts` (`classifyNoteContentType` → `stripCommentAnchors`): linear, golden and doubling tests, plus the bounded 2,000-blank test that was [red first](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45034951410) at 2.3 to 6.7 s.
- `packages/editor/src/mount.tsx` (`extractCommentAnchorIds` on every save's snapshot): linear, same tests.
- `packages/editor/src/selection.ts` and `packages/viewer/src/selection.ts` (`stripCommentMarkerTokens` on a selection's lines): linear, same tests.
- `vendor/.../renderer/panels/CanvasAreaContent.tsx` (`extractCommentAnchorIds` when a web-app save prunes comments) and `editor/utils/comment-thread-count.ts` (comment counts): linear, same tests. Before, a collaborator's `{%c:` and a few thousand blanks froze every other tab that saved or counted comments on the note.
- `vendor/.../renderer/editor/MarkdownEditor.tsx` (`containsCommentAnchors` on pasted text): linear, same tests.
- `editor/utils/comment-markdown.ts`: re-exports the functions above; its other marker users (`detectCommentSyntax`, and in the two common files `stripCommentAnchorsForIds`, `findCommentMarkerIds`, `hasLegacyCommentMarkers`, `hasModernCommentMarkers`) have no caller in the vendored tree or the web app, so no text another person wrote reaches them; they still run the regexes.
- `apps/web/src/host/history/HistoryView.tsx`, `packages/viewer/src/moss-file.ts`, `packages/sync/src/doc-do.ts`, `push.ts`, `title-line.ts`, `editor/plugins/FileLinkPlugin.tsx`, `editor/utils/noteFrontmatter.ts`: only `splitFrontmatter` and `disassembleNote`, no marker regex; the frontmatter pattern is anchored at the start, so linear.
- `editor/markdown/normalize.ts`: imports only a type.

### T9.47 — what a peer's block change still does to your caret

Each is the behaviour before T9.47 unless it says otherwise, and each has a test that pins it; none is fixed by T9.47.

1. Peers can see your fallback caret for one frame: the binding publishes it while it applies the peer's change, and the corrected position follows in the same task. Removing that frame needs the awareness positions rewritten before the binding syncs, a vendored seam (j01-caret J9 asserts the settled state).
2. Keys still on their way to the server when a peer replaces your block are lost with it, or stay in the first half when the peer splits it before your caret; your caret is placed on your words regardless (j01-caret-flight K1-K6, peer-caret.test.ts U4). A limit of the stored layout.
3. While an input method composition is open nothing is restored. No real input method has been run against this (peer-caret.test.ts C8).
4. With the body not focused, the editor's selection is restored and the browser's is not; whether a later selection change from the browser overrides it is unverified (j01-caret-keep M4 checks focus only).
5. Suggest and Review: the module is not registered.
6. Undo: the first key after a restore starts a new undo step, as a click would (j01-caret-keep M8). Undo-step carets, comment anchors and suggestion marks that name the replaced block are not repaired.
7. Merges and moves: a peer's Backspace-merge, a peer's undo of their Enter, a tick that sorts your check list, any block move (j01-caret-keep M1-M3, peer-caret.test.ts C1, C2, U8).
8. A peer's bold or link before your caret in the same text moves it (peer-caret.test.ts C1).
9. Refused on purpose: a conversion that re-creates the lines around yours in the same update: a whole list changed between bullets and numbers, a bullet that joins the lists above and below into one, or several of a co-editor's edits delivered together after a reconnect; a commit that also changed the line above; a peer's multi-paragraph paste into your line; a commit that changed your line on both sides of the caret; your Backspaces in flight across a peer's Enter; a peer's Enter and their typing at your place in one commit; inserted text the two-position test cannot place; a third person's unsent characters on your right inside a split tail; your own Backspaces bringing your caret exactly to the peer's split point (peer-caret.test.ts C2-C7); a peer's Markdown shortcut (`# `, `> `, `- `) typed at the start of your line while your own typed keys or Backspaces are in flight, since your line then differs from the converted block at both ends (peer-caret-text.test.ts).
10. A range end that was not lost keeps its old offset even if the same commit shifted it.
11. A conversion larger than 256 KiB arrives in pieces with its deletes last, so the delete and the insert land in different commits and nothing is restored.
12. A local Lexical update still pending when the peer's change arrives shares its commit; not shown reachable from the socket path, and not handled.
13. Accepted by design: when your line is replaced on its own, the one new line in its place takes your caret whenever its text begins or ends like your line, even if a co-editor deleted your paragraph and wrote that line fresh.
14. When several of a co-editor's edits reach you in one update (after a reconnect, or a resend of their merged edits) and the lines next to yours read alike, your caret can land in their new line instead of on your words: for example, with two lines that both read "T" and your caret in the second, their deletion that joins the two lines plus an Enter at the end puts your caret in the new empty line below.

### T9.51 — what the unsaved-text card still does

T9.51 fixed only the first press on the card's buttons with the caret in the note. Each of these is about the same card, and none is fixed by T9.51.

Ways the card can still cost a person their text:

1. Dismiss does not ask and cannot be undone, and it works before any copy has (`UnsavedCard.tsx`, `suggest/mode.ts`). A product question for the owner.
2. Corrected once T5.S16 is under it: a reload no longer drops the card unasked. While offered-back text waits, the browser asks before the page leaves (`unacked.ts`), and so does every full load the app starts through `navigation.ts`, such as Sign in on an ended session; sign-out and Reload from the update notice and the app menu ask first with their own confirm, and the stale-chunk reload waits. The text is still lost once the person chooses to leave, since it lives only in that window.
3. A paragraph identical to another unsaved one is kept once (`packages/sync/src/suggest/client.ts`, a `Set`).
4. An empty paragraph typed while offline is left out (the same line).
5. Markdown punctuation comes back escaped, such as `snake\_case`, `5 \* 3` and `back\\slash` (`suggest/mounts.ts` exports Markdown), though the card's comment says "as typed". Whether pasting into Moss restores the characters is not known. Items 3 to 5 are in `client.ts`, the same file on m5 to m9, so their fix belongs on m5.
6. A press on the card's words still moves the card when the caret is in the note (from the code, not seen), so the first try at selecting the text by hand, or at dragging its scrollbar, starts on a card that drops. That is where the card sends people when the clipboard refuses, and after T9.51 a refused Copy leaves the caret in the note.
7. The card gives back whole paragraphs and nothing marks which words were not saved; someone else's words in that paragraph come back with it (from the code).
8. A refusal that arrives after the person left the note stores the text, but the card shows only in that note's pane and nothing elsewhere says text is waiting (`suggest/mounts.ts`, `pane.tsx`).
9. "Copied" never goes back while the card stays up, so it does not follow a later change of the clipboard (`UnsavedCard.tsx`).

Things about the card that do not lose text by themselves:

10. While the note is locked out, the connection notice leads and the card is behind "Recover unsaved text (N)".
11. When the leading notice changes, or another note is opened and this one reopened, the text stays but the shown text collapses and "Copied" is forgotten.
12. A second refusal adds its own row; it does not replace the first.
13. Long text is not cut: it sits in a 160 px box that scrolls, with nothing marking that there is more below.
14. The card is still drawn while History is open or the note is in Trash (from the code). In Trash it is drawn in a second region (`NoticeStack.tsx`) that does not get T9.51's handler; that region does not follow the toolbar, so the first-press fault is not expected there. Not run.
15. A press already in flight when the toolbar comes or goes for another reason (text selected in the note, a rebuild putting the caret back) can still miss (from the code).

The lower branches, m5 to m8 (from the code; their band was never pressed in a browser):

16. Copy is the only way back to the text; there is no Show text.
17. Nothing is offered if the clipboard refuses, and with no clipboard API the press does nothing.
18. Dismiss drops every closed suggestion's text at once, with no question.

Not known: Firefox, Safari on macOS and touch; Copy outside headless Chromium's own clipboard (what WebKit's clipboard holds, a headed browser, a paste into another app); the card when the clipboard refuses, and its no-room form; T9.51's handler on the region's other buttons with the caret in the note (only Retry and the card's three actions were pressed); block kinds other than paragraphs and one bullet list; three or more closed suggestions, text beyond about 1,000 characters and panes narrower than 1440 x 1000; how often a refusal happens in real use.

### T9.53 — not in this task

None of these is fixed or proven by T9.53.

- **A share link shows your own note (older than m9).** A signed-in person with a note of her own who opens a view link to someone else's note sees her own most recently changed note, the address becomes `/d/<her note>?share=<token>`, and the shared note never opens. Signed out, the same link opens the shared note. Unchanged by T9.53, which adds one window to this route: until the access read settles (at most 10 s) no focus is reported, so a note clicked then is written by a replace, not pushed as a visit. Likely cause, not proven: startup selection stops at `if (store.get(activeNoteIdAtom))` in App.tsx, true because of the fallback to the newest note; a likely fix is to test the stored choice (`activeNoteSelectionAtom`) there. Needs its own task and leg; every share-link leg in j08 opens the link as a person with no notes.
- Other routes with the same cause, expected to be covered by the fix but never run with it: a note in a second vault opened by its address while the stored vault is the first (seen without the fix); a note shared by a grant opened by its address; a folder address `/f/<id>`; returning from sign-in to `/d/<id>`; Open in New Window; a bell notice about a folder; Back after a reload to an earlier note; a collaborator's edit changing which note is the newest.
- A note trashed while one Back away: Back shows it in Trash and rewrites its entry to the last live note, so the history holds that note twice and one later Forward changes nothing on screen; restoring the note does not bring its entry back.
- Moss still mounts the newest note for one render at every startup (its panes report comes 3 to 5 ms before the addressed note's); whether its socket opens then is not on record, and the legs allow for it.
- A slow list could make Moss's first focus report "no note"; the host returns early on it. No log shows one.
- Not on record: what switching vault in the app, or choosing a search result from another vault, writes to the history.
- Not known: whether j13-vaults' "the notice says why" fails more often in WebKit with the fix; the fix on staging or with real network delay; WebKit driven by hand.

Evidence recorded with the review of 2026-11-27:

- Red first: the j13-vault-nav leg on b8bbf4c7, before the fix ([run 45446874324](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45446874324): Chromium job 138634303579, WebKit job 138634303645).
- The T9.19 and T9.46 legs with the fix, on the merged build b79fe0c4, which also holds T9.50's fix of the Share role-word leg: the selection `T9.53|T9.19|T9.46` at repeat 3, twice in both engines ([run 45970878040](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45970878040), [run 45970908181](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45970908181)). Chromium passed 57 of 57 in each run; WebKit passed 57 of 57 in the second run and 56 of 57 in the first.
- The one failure: one WebKit execution of a T9.19 leg, `j06-folders.spec.ts:650` "Back through two notes trashed since she left them shows each in Trash, and the address keeps naming the live note" (job 140411640700), failed once in teardown with invariant 3: 2 socket opens of one note in one document, 1 allowed, both closed normally, and no engine crash. Nothing was re-run.
- The diagnosis: the fix did not cause it and does not make it more likely. It is a fault in the leg's setup, on m9 since 2026-11-22. While the leg creates its three notes through the API, the window, where no note is chosen, can pick the second note when the third create is slow and remember it; after the reload it opens that note by itself, the leg opens it again, and the leg allows a second open only for the third note. Neither Back opened a socket. A person sees nothing wrong: the window shows the note it had picked rather than the newest, which is T9.48a's rule.
- How that was measured. The leg alone in WebKit at repeat 10, six dispatches on each of three commits, failed on none: m9 at 0578360f without the fix 0 of 60 ([46014324727](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014324727), [46014382692](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014382692), [46014440298](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014440298), [46015636465](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015636465), [46015698580](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015698580), [46015757842](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015757842)); T9.53's head 7d2c3254 0 of 60 ([46014344502](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014344502), [46014401230](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014401230), [46014459135](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014459135), [46015656822](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015656822), [46015717490](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015717490), [46015777862](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015777862)); the merged build b79fe0c4 0 of 60 ([46014363757](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014363757), [46014420336](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014420336), [46014478753](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46014478753), [46015679269](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015679269), [46015737325](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015737325), [46015798170](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015798170)). With the earlier runs counted ([45973846164](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45973846164), [45973862040](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45973862040)), WebKit totals are m9 0 of 60, T9.53's head 0 of 70 and the merged build 1 of 76. Plain repeats cannot separate the commits, so the comparison rests on a probe of the leg on two throwaway branches of m9, one without the fix and one with it, each in both engines at repeat 10. Recording only, 0 of 10 failed in each engine on both branches; holding the third create until the window has remembered a note, the same invariant failed on the same note 10 of 10 in each engine on both branches ([46015376050](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015376050) without the fix, [46015395571](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015395571) with it). The same hold with `/` opened instead of a reload: 10 of 10 in each engine on both ([46016989825](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46016989825) without, [46017009528](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46017009528) with). A pause of 120 to 165 ms before the third create: 36 of 40 failed without the fix, 38 of 40 with it ([46020871791](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46020871791) and [46020911467](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46020911467) without, [46020889994](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46020889994) and [46020934312](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46020934312) with). The two T9.48a legs passed 80 of 80 in both engines ([46015042705](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015042705) on T9.53's head, [46015063231](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46015063231) on the merged build).
- The fault is not the fix's, and it has its own task: a test fix in the leg on m9, not in the harness or the product. After the reload the leg reads the one note the window opened by itself, waits for it to be live and gives the one extra open to that note instead of to the third note; the sibling leg at `:551` gets the same change. Its red-first leg is the probe's hold variant, red 10 of 10 in each engine on m9 without the fix.
- Until that task merges, this leg is a known intermittent failure on m9: `j06-folders.spec.ts:650` in WebKit, invariant 3 with 2 socket opens of one note, at 1 failure in 206 plain WebKit executions across m9, T9.53's head and the merged build (Chromium passed its 6 plain executions on the merged build).

### T9.52a — the hibernation leg's cold peer is its own person

- The j04 leg "reopen and warm creator with a cold peer" gave one person both notes. After the idle, that person's reopen page read the warm note (`GET /api/docs/<warm>/content`, from the formula index, plus its backlinks and `/api/workspace?doc=<warm>`) and woke it before the cold peer arrived. The new check "only the cold peer asks for the warm note after the idle" was red 5 of 5 in each engine before the fix ([run 45423944384](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45423944384)). With the warm note shared with a third person it passed 10 of 10 in each engine over two dispatches ([45426881415](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45426881415), [45426903295](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45426903295)); the warm note's instance was built 646 to 790 ms after the cold peer's action began in every execution.
- Not covered any more: no leg now has one person listing two notes across an idle, so no test shows that a page load reads, and so wakes, every note the person can list.
- Not covered: no leg covers a note woken by that read while a hidden socket to it survives, with a peer joining after. That path passed 12 of 12 when it was measured with the two failing checks made soft, and `docs/history/LEARNINGS.md` records a past defect on it. Both points wait on the owner's ruling on page-load reads.

### T9.52c — a space typed in a title is stored as a space

- The title field was `white-space: normal`, so the engine put in U+00A0 for a space typed first, last or beside another space, and the field stored the element's text as it was; a word pasted after a typed space (the title's own `onPaste`) kept it inside the title, where the title filter, Trash search and the CLI's title match miss it. The field is now `white-space: pre-wrap` (`apps/web/src/styles.css`, DEVIATIONS 39). The new j02 leg (` world` typed first; `2026` pasted after `Meeting `) was red 5 of 5 in each engine before the rule, in both cases ([run 45446892415](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45446892415), jobs 138633780253 Chromium, 138633780130 WebKit). With the fold in `nonMonotonic` removed and no rule, "renames from Ada and then Ben reach Cy in order" was red 5 of 5 in each engine in the same run: it now guards the rule. With the rule the whole `j02-title` file (21 legs) passed 10 of 10 in each engine over two dispatches ([45451450344](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45451450344), [45451456917](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45451456917)), with no fold left.
- Not changed: titles that already hold U+00A0 stay as they are; nothing rewrites stored titles.
- Not changed: a U+00A0 inside pasted text is kept.
- Not changed: deleting the middle word of `a b c` now leaves two plain spaces where it left a space and a U+00A0, so a search for "a c" still misses.
- Not reached: the editor package's own title (`packages/editor/src/mount.tsx`, `data-moss-editor-title`) does not carry `data-title-binding`.
- On screen, in Chromium (WebKit could not be driven locally; its CI legs are the evidence): two spaces inside a title and a pasted tab are drawn as typed in the title field, where they collapsed before; the sidebar, tab, breadcrumb and viewer heading still collapse them. A cleared title still shows its placeholder. A title holding a line break, which only the rename route or the CLI can store, is drawn on two lines and the title grows from 48 to 72 px, pushing the body down 24 px; before, it read as one line. Shift+Enter and Alt+Enter in a title move to the body, as Enter does, before and after the rule: the title holds only the word typed before them, 48 px high, so no key makes a two-line title.
- Possible refinement, not tried: a newer CSS value that keeps spaces and still draws a line break as a space (`white-space-collapse: preserve-spaces`) would keep a stored line break on one line, but it is not available in every browser a person may use.

### T9.54h — a share link opens the shared note for someone with notes of their own

- Implemented, awaiting the checker. Signed in with a note of their own, a person who opened a link to someone else's note was shown their own newest note at `owner`, under the link's address, and their typing landed in it. The route gate now keeps its open answer once, and before moss mounts the boot puts that note into moss's note store as the note the tab is on. The note gets no sidebar row. Five host files change: `host/access.ts`, `host/boot.tsx`, `host/bridge/index.ts`, `host/moss-modules.d.ts`, `routes/d.$docId.tsx`. No vendored file, server file, on-screen word or deviation row changes.
- Red first, legs only (`j08-link-notes`, seven legs, its own `linkNotes` group): [run 45629719985](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45629719985), 3 repeats, Chromium job 139259873208 and WebKit job 139259873261. Legs 1, 2 (both widths), 3, 4, 5 and 7 were red in every repeat in both engines where the brief says; leg 5 was red 3 of 3 in each engine at "no note had a sidebar row at any moment"; leg 6 was green.
- With the fix as first drafted, where a failed listing read made the boot stand back: [run 45633787313](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45633787313), 3 repeats, jobs 139274311509 and 139274311817. Leg 4 was red in every repeat in both engines, with another note in the pane; every other leg was green.
- With the fix (a listing read that fails concludes nothing): [run 45636500098](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45636500098), all seven legs green 10 of 10 in each engine (jobs 139283936200, 139283936342), and no WebKit log line "WebSocket is closed before the connection is established". `j08-share` and `j08-agents` green in both engines ([run 45636505494](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45636505494)); `j09-revoke-live`, `j10-stranger-phone` and `j13-vaults` green in both engines ([run 45636510948](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45636510948)).
- `checks` (Typecheck, Lint, Unit tests, Vendor drift, Shard budget) green on the fix commit, [run 45636501508](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45636501508), after one re-run: its first attempt failed `sharing-security.test.ts` "settles concurrent shares of one person at every role on the highest" on an assertion inside miniflare's proxy worker, a server test this change does not touch.
- Nothing was driven by hand in WebKit; WebKit evidence is the CI legs only.

What the change does not do:

- The linked note gets no row, and no row is highlighted while it is open. After a click on one of their own notes, the way back is moss's back arrow.
- A revoked link does not hide the note: the pane ends in place with "Your access to this note has ended.", the title and last text stay readable, and the role shown is unchanged. Every request is still checked by the server.
- Moss's remembered note becomes the linked note, so the next plain `/` opens the newest note instead of an older remembered one. Inferred, not run.
- A look at Trash and back lands on the person's newest note, not the linked note. Inferred.
- It is not faster: while the listing is read the tab shows the host's blank boot frame instead of moss's.

Not in this task:

- On `m9`, the browser's Back to the linked note shows its sidebar row for about 185 to 260 ms. Back hands the note to moss through its "open this note" path, which lists it until the next full listing read. It needs its own task and a red leg.
- Whether a note opened through a link should have a lasting row in the opener's sidebar (open owner question). This fix works without one.
- "Copy note link" and "Open in New Window" on the person's own note carry the link's token.
- Inferred and never run: both listing reads failing; a failed first listing for the owner or a grant holder; the token revoked between the gate and the socket; a person whose only listed note is shared by a grant; a note in a linked folder opened by its address; a second vault of the person's own; the owner trashing the linked note while it is open this way; a linked note whose formulas refer to its own named formulas.
- `m4` to `m8` are not built or run with this change.

On the way up:

| Branch | Product files | The legs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `m3` | The five edits | As built here |
| `m4` to `m7` | Nothing to adapt (inferred): `boot.tsx`, `bridge/index.ts` and the route match `m3`; `access.ts` and `moss-modules.d.ts` merge cleanly. Not built or run | Unchanged |
| `m8` | The same; the merge is clean. Not built or run | Unchanged |
| `m9` | The same five files, after T9.53 is in `m9` | Their `m9` form: the sign-in control is "Sign in", and the history check forbids any other note in the address |

## T9.55d A click on a suggestion's wedge survives a repaint

- **The defect.** In Edit mode every repaint emptied the marks overlay and built new wedges, gutter bars and dots, and every key a co-editor types repaints the owner's note. A browser sends `click` only when press and release land on the same element, so a held click on a wedge opened nothing whenever a repaint landed inside it. Measured on `m9`, presses held 120 ms or more: a quiet note 24 of 24 opened in each engine; the suggester still typing, 18 of 24 (WebKit) and 6 of 24 (Chromium); another editor typing in another paragraph, 0 of 24 in each. Every lost click had its wedge replaced between press and release (75 of 75); none without a replacement was lost (141 of 141).
- **What the test runs saw.** The review shard failed five times under three messages from this one cause: `locator.hover: Element is not visible` on the proposals leg (once, WebKit), `[data-suggestions-panel]` never found after the wedge click in "two clicks on a marker never reject" (three times, WebKit), and an empty preview style read on the proposals leg (once). The specs had been patched around the replacement three times.
- **The identity.** A mark keeps its element while its identity stands: an insert by its record and the Yjs id of its anchor character, a payload mark by its record and the decorator's Yjs id, a block dot by its record and the block's Yjs id, a gutter bar by its record and its block element. None of these moves when someone types elsewhere, earlier in the same paragraph, or adds a block above, unlike a text node offset or a block index. The elements are held in a map beside the overlay (no new attribute), each click handler is bound once and opens its own record, and the overlay stays in mark order, moving a kept element only when its order changes. The reopen path for replaced wedges is gone, and with it T5.B3's piling document listeners.
- **The proof.** A `drawMarks` unit test with two suggestions that keep their elements, each opening its own record, while text is typed before one, a block is added above both, their order swaps and one grows, and whose closed one leaves; two j16-marks legs (a co-editor's key and repaint inside a held press; five keys typed earlier in the wedge's own paragraph). Red before on `m5`, green after; the review leg's wedge click is no longer forced. A look at a local stack with a co-editor typing about 8 keys a second: 0 of 11 held clicks opened before, 11 of 11 after (8 in light, 3 in dark); the preview under the pointer was read for the 8 light clicks after (open, reading `plus`), not for the 3 dark ones. The full lane on that head had 79 jobs succeed and `macos` skipped.
- **The preview under a resting pointer.** A third j16-marks leg: Ada hovers the wedge (unforced) and keeps the pointer still while Ben types ten more characters into his suggestion one at a time; after each key reaches Ada, one read (no retry) finds the preview shown with the current text, and at the end the wedge is the same element. 10 of 10 in each engine on this head ([run 45698182960](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45698182960)). On the commit before the change with the leg added, 0 of 10 in each engine, each failing at the first key with the wedge's element replaced ([run 45697150045](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45697150045)). A look at a local stack (Chromium, light and dark) caught the preview under the resting pointer while Ben typed, then a held click of about 135 ms opened his card.
- **Not measured.** Real hands and real typists, touch, and clicks on the gutter bar and the attribute dot (bound the same way, inferred to be fixed the same way).

## T9.55 on m8: the recording control and the rebind fixture

**The recording control (T9.55f).** `selftest/recording.spec.ts`'s control ("against a loopback target, the checkpoint and its failure capture the page") failed about 1 run in 350 in Chromium (23 of 8,041 executions from 2026-11-13 to 11-25; 26 in about 10,044 since the selftest arrived) with `page.screenshot: Protocol error (Page.captureScreenshot): Unable to capture screenshot`, and as a selftest it then stopped its shard's journeys (288 legs in 12 days).
- Cause: the selftest checks only whether the harness asks for a capture, yet its wrapper passed each call through to Chromium. Chromium refused the first capture, asked 10 to 20 ms after `goto`, when the image it got was empty; that the page had not yet painted its first frame is inferred from one live refusal.
- Untested lead for why the control's place is about 50 times likelier than the same steps elsewhere: it runs straight after two tests that load the same page, and Playwright captures that page when their context closes.
- With `screenshot: 'only-on-failure'`, Playwright calls `page.screenshot` on every page at each `context.close()`, pass or fail, and swallows any refusal. That call reaches the selftest's wrapper too; the wrapper writes no file, so a recording selftest that fails for another reason has no Playwright failure screenshot.
- Now the wrapper counts the call and returns a fixed buffer. The three tests keep every assertion. The control's time fell to that of the two no-capture tests (Chromium 96 ms beside 94 and 88 ms; before, about 430 to 580 ms beside about 200).
- No selftest now sends a real capture through `Actors`. Once the checkpoint's capture guard is carried up from `m3`, a refusal in a journey shows only as a `capture-skipped` annotation on the test, and the count of those is the number to watch.

**The `rebind-keeps-text` fixture (T9.55h).** The positive control for invariant 7's wait on a rebinding field rebound after 200 ms with no input. A pause between two keys (macOS WebKit stalls 0.7 to 1.0 s between keys in most typings) fired it mid-typing: the body unbound, the rest of the keys went nowhere, and the selftest read `"Quick fox, wörld… é!" appears 0 time(s), typed 1`. A stall case (five characters, 300 ms, the rest) was red on the old fixture in both Linux engines with that message. The fixture now rebinds when the selftest calls `window.rebind()` after typing. `rebind-loses-text` and `stuck-unbound` keep their timers: they expect a finding, so a stall cannot fail them.

### T9.50 — seven intermittent failures, first pass (c, d, e, f)

All four are faults in the tests or their readouts; none was the product misbehaving. Runs are in brsbl/moss-multi.

- c, the owner's Trash notice: in the owner's window the collaborator's "This note was moved to Trash." card is a passing state that Moss's own Trash notice replaces. Proof on a throwaway branch: with the owner's notice asserted first, the old check for the card failed 6 of 6 in Chromium and 6 of 6 in WebKit ([run 45347761953](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45347761953)). j13-vaults and j06-folders now wait for the owner's notice, keep the card for the collaborator and require one notice in each window.
- d, the Share role menu: a mouse press opens the menu on the next frame, so an Escape a few milliseconds after the click closed the dialog instead. Proof: the one-shot row read was empty in 6 of 10 Chromium repeats ([run 45347810864](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45347810864)). The leg now waits for the menu and its five role words, checks Escape closed the menu alone, and waits for the invites list rather than the header.
- e, the reviewer's preview: not built. The section's forcing experiment (hold Ada's first preview request until her reject has answered) gave no 409 on Ada: 3 of 3 ([run 45347891748](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45347891748)), then 5 of 5 with her reject held until her preview had gone out, which it had in 4 ([run 45350214007](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45350214007)). The cause: since T8.F, merged into m9 by T9.W after the failure was recorded, a card aborts its own preview request when its review starts and none waits (`previews.drop`), so the request no longer reaches the page as a 409. The leg keeps rejecting at once, which exercises that abort. Ben's declared 409 stays.
- f, the suggest readouts: `data-suggest-sent` and `data-suggest-refused` reached the DOM at React's next commit, after `data-sync-unacked` went to 0 in the tick of the acknowledgement. The pane now writes both in that same tick, only while it is in Suggest; the render still gives the first value and removes them when the pane leaves Suggest. While `data-sync-unacked` reads 1 a late render can still briefly write an older count; tests read the counts only after it reads 0. The new readouts leg (`e2e/journeys/j16-readouts.spec.ts`, in the suggestMarks shard because the suggest shard's WebKit estimate is full) was red 10 of 10 in both engines before the change, recording a sent count of 0 where the pane kept 1 ([run 45348180927](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45348180927)). No e2e read of the counters changed.
- Repeats on 53b06cce: the j13 and j06 trash legs, the Share role-word leg, the rebuilt-fork leg and the readouts leg 10 of 10 each in both engines ([run 45349536321](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45349536321)); `j16-paste` (with j16-paste-cells and j16-paste-undo) 160 of 160 in Chromium ([run 45349549866](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45349549866)).
- The c and d repeats grep the changed legs, not the whole j13-vaults and j06-folders files: 17 legs at ten repeats would pass the 25-minute shard limit in WebKit.
- Section c's repeats, completed on the reviewed head 1daf42a3 as a later ruling ordered: all 17 legs of `j13-vaults` (4) and `j06-folders` (13), in dispatches of two or three legs selected by title, each passed 20 times in WebKit and 10 times in Chromium, with no failed execution and nothing re-run.
