### T9.50 — seven intermittent failures, second pass (g, h, i)

All three are faults in the tests or their scripts; none was the product misbehaving.

- g, Node-side test requests: Playwright sends API requests through process-wide keep-alive agents, and the local Worker ignores a request's `connection: close`, so every request rode a pooled socket and one written as the Worker closed it 5 s after its last answer failed with "socket hang up". No Node HTTP or HTTPS agent in a test worker now keeps a socket alive (`ownConnections` in `e2e/lib/infra.ts`, called at module scope by both fixtures). `closeEachConnection` and T8.F's `freshConnections` in `e2e/lib/actors.ts`, which added only the header, are removed. Nothing is resent, so a "socket hang up" now means the stack dropped a request. The cost is one TLS handshake per Node-side request against a deployed stack; Node `fetch` helpers are unchanged. The selftest's server now answers as the Worker does (no `Connection: close`, socket left open) and counts a GET, a POST and a `fetch` through `request.newContext()` and a browser context's request: 4 requests on 1 connection in both engines before ([run 45362410806](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45362410806)), 4 on 4 after. Repeats after, all passed with no "socket hang up" in any log: `j01-caret-flight` (8 legs) at repeat 10 on two dispatches in WebKit, 80 of 80 each ([run 45363206382](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45363206382), which also ran it 80 of 80 in Chromium, and [run 45412204558](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45412204558)); `j01-registers` (40 legs) at repeat 2 on four dispatches in WebKit, 80 of 80 each ([run 45363216953](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45363216953) and [run 45363232423](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45363232423) with `j01-caret-flight`, [run 45412223089](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45412223089), [run 45412236639](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45412236639)), so 8 runs of each registers leg where the section asked for 20. Repeat 10 of `j01-registers` in WebKit (about 5 minutes a repeat) would pass the 18-minute suite limit, so its repeats are 2 per dispatch.
- h, the memory script: workerd closes a kept-alive connection 5 s after its last use on a clock that stands still while JavaScript runs, and the table jobs send two multi-second requests back to back. Every request of `scripts/measure-memory.mjs` (the ping, `call`, the refused create, `/state`) goes through one helper that asks for its connection to close; `scripts/measure-converter.mjs` sends the same header through its own helper. A failed request is rethrown as `<path>: <message> (<cause code>)`, and a failed run records the milliseconds since the previous answer and since `/create` was sent, and workerd's exit code and signal after waiting up to 10 s for its exit. No retry, timeout or budget changed. The unit test (three calls in a row, each with `connection: close`, on three sockets, each closed before the next request arrived) was red before ([run 45363301376](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45363301376): the requests asked for `keep-alive`), green after. The ceiling table job failed once more with `create: fetch failed` on the commit before the fix (same run). After the fix both table jobs passed 9 of 9 executions in every `checks` run: [run 45368855430](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45368855430) attempt 1, [run 45374402232](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45374402232), [run 45374709057](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45374709057) and [run 45374781041](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45374781041) with each table job run three times, and the full lane [run 45382184674](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45382184674). One more ceiling execution (run 45368855430 attempt 2) was cancelled after 23 s by the next push, before it measured anything.
- i, stored media: `toEqual` on two 12 MiB `Uint8Array`s walks them element by element and then deep-compares `Object.entries` of both. Uploaded bodies in `apps/web/src/api/assets.test.ts` are compared by length and `Buffer.compare`, naming the first differing offset on a mismatch, and the two video tests lost their 60 s limit. Before ([run 45363301376](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45363301376)): 'stores a video of several segments in one put' 57,220 ms, 'leaves nothing stored when a video put is cut off' 50,215 ms, the file 249,166 ms. After ([run 45368855430](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45368855430)): 1,278 ms, 1,355 ms, the file 142,652 ms.
- Outside these sections: the full lane on 2dcfe28c ([run 45382184674](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45382184674) attempt 1) failed once in `e2e (webkit, workspace)` at `export.spec.ts:67` with "Target crashed": the reader's page process aborted on `GLib:ERROR … g_main_context_check_unlocked: assertion failed` (WebKit's stderr in `engine.json`) as Save as PDF opened its tab. It is the recurring `export.spec.ts` page-process fault T9.48g counted, not touched here; the failed job passed on re-run.

### T9.50 — two product defects found in review (a, b)

- a, one notice on a note trashed under its owner: the pane moved its notices out of the bottom slot as soon as the bridge's listing knew the note was trashed, before Moss's own record did, so the owner's note could show no notice until Moss drew its Trash notice. Where the notices stand now follows Moss's record (`note.trashedAt`); the binding, the Trash view and `gone` still follow the bridge. A notice watch (`e2e/lib/notice-watch.ts`, a MutationObserver) records every moment a page that has shown a notice holds none or two; j13-vaults runs it on the owner through a vault trash with Escape pressed as soon as the listing after the trash answers, and j06-folders through a folder trash. Before the change the j13 watch recorded a moment with no notice in 5 of 10 WebKit repeats ([run 45426865441](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45426865441)). After it both watches were empty 20 of 20 in WebKit ([run 45433647372](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45433647372), [run 45433669076](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45433669076)) and 10 of 10 in Chromium ([run 45429027533](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45429027533), whose WebKit shard ran the whole two files and passed the suite's 18-minute limit, with no failure in what it ran). Step 3's vendor patch was not needed. Left for the owner to decide:
  - the owner still reads the collaborator's wording, "This note was moved to Trash.", until Moss's record catches up; it is one notice throughout, with a change of words;
  - a trashed note whose content fails to load has no Trash notice (Moss's own condition, `!contentLoadError`);
  - the Trash view branch of the pane is untouched;
  - when the owner moves her own note to Trash in this window, the collaborator's wording stands for one listing round trip (the trash's answer until the listing after it answers) before her own notice replaces it.
- b, Share names the note: a note's dialog was headed "Note" alone until its three reads (members, links, invites) had all answered, and for good if one failed. It now starts with the title of Moss's note record, the one the window shows, trimmed; an empty title or Moss's "Untitled" placeholder gives "Note" alone. The members read still replaces it when it answers, and a failed read leaves it. Folder and vault dialogs are unchanged. The new j08-share leg holds the three reads: before the change the header read "Note" while they were held, in both engines ([run 45429180636](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45429180636)); after it the leg passed 10 of 10 in both engines ([run 45433828485](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45433828485)). The first fix seeded Moss's "Untitled" as a name and the leg's untitled step caught it ([run 45430325634](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45430325634)). The other legs that open Share already wait on lists or retrying assertions, not the header, so none changed.
- b, on reopening: the dialog stays mounted while closed, so Share reopened on the same note kept the name of its last opening (the old title after a rename until the members read answered, and for good if a read failed; "Note" alone for a note titled since). The name is now seeded again from Moss's note record each time the dialog opens, by the same rule. A second j08-share leg renames a note between two openings and titles an untitled one, with the reads held: red before in both engines ([run 45447064416](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45447064416): header `Note “Packing list”` where `Note “Packing list for June”` was expected), 10 of 10 in both engines after ([run 45448653692](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45448653692)), and the whole of j08 green once in both ([run 45448674902](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45448674902)).

### T9.55a — the undo leg's click

- Cause: Lexical takes the first `selectionchange` after a pointer press as the click's own; when the leg typed 23 to 32 ms after its click, the first character was handled before that event, a selection-only update followed it, and Moss's history rule (T9.13) began a new step at the second character. Ada's stack then held three steps (the split, the space, "ADA"), so the leg's two undos left the paragraph split.
- Rate: 10 of 104 first-attempt Chromium coedit jobs in full lanes on commits with T9.13 failed, every one on this leg; 0 of 39 without T9.13. Alone at repeat 10 the unchanged leg failed 3, 1 and 6 of 10 (runs 45613272650, 45613284047, 45613298056), and 1 of 10 again on 2026-11-25 with the same received text (run 45649176105, job 139329442166).
- The product question was answered no (run 45614600675): one Cmd+Z after Ada's split, with Ben typing in the moved half, made the paragraph whole and kept " BEN" on both screens 10 of 10; in 30 executions where Ada also typed, the undo after her typing restored the paragraph every time (second press 19, third press 11), the peers converged after every press, and no undo was lost, skipped or applied to Ben's text.
- The fix: `caretAtEnd` polls `_inputState.isSelectionChangeFromMouseDown` after its click until it is false, or 500 ms. With it the leg passed 30 of 30 (runs 45648147399, 45648162936, 45648175807; jobs 139325748591, 139325765871, 139325705555) in 11.7-12.2, 10.6-10.8 and 11.7-12.2 s, against 10.3-10.4 s for the unchanged leg on the same day. A probe of the wait (20 calls of Ada's "First half" click) ended at once 18 times and after 8 and 20 ms twice, the race it removes; Ben's click on " BEN" moves nothing and ends on the 500 ms bound every time, which accounts for half a second of each leg.
- Not fixed, the same in pristine Moss: a click where the caret already is fires no `selectionchange`, so the next typed character is an undo step of its own (6 of 6 in each, run 45616489065). It costs one extra Cmd+Z and loses nothing. The three legs that use `pressUntil` type right after a `caretAtEnd`; whether their clicks move the caret is not known, and they were left alone.

### T9.55c — WebKit's libsoup check, labelled

- Finding (Playwright 1.61.1 WebKit, WPE build 2311, Linux): j09's "reloading while the note's access read is in flight" leg fails in WebKit about once in 56 executions. As a reload cancels the departing page's module loads, the browser's stderr carries `libsoup-CRITICAL … soup_session_feature_request_queued` (or `_unqueued`) `: assertion 'SOUP_IS_SESSION_FEATURE (feature)' failed`; 2.5 to 2.8 s later every load of the new page fails with "WebKit encountered an internal error" and the page stays blank. It is the open upstream WebKit bug 276819 ("WPENetworkProcess segfaults occasionally after logging assertion errors", status NEW, no patch); its comments point at libsoup merge request 387 and GLib issue 1346. Nothing in the repository can remove it, and the leg is right.
- The label: Playwright's `[err]` line of that text is noted with the time the log received it, beside the log ring (`e2e/lib/engine.ts`); `Actors.settle` reports a failed WebKit test BLOCKED with the fixed name when such a line was noted after its `Actors` was made, with the invariants' message beneath. It keys on time, not on context. A passing test with the line in its window adds one `engine-fault-survived` annotation with the count, so the record shows how often the engine survives the assertion. "WebKit encountered an internal error" alone, and a failure with no such line, stay FAILED. The summary prints only the fixed name; two such rows in one job at repeat 1 read FAILED.
- What BLOCKED means here (docs/METHOD.md): run it again once; BLOCKED again on the same test is a finding to report, not a third run. No automatic retry.
- Signature: of 253 failed WebKit test results since 2026-11-22 that carry the browser's log (about 988 contexts), the line is in 2, both this leg. Runs re-run to green were not scanned.
- Selftests red in [run 45659803473](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45659803473) (jobs 139371045491 and 139371045603, on the assertions), green in [run 45662029846](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45662029846) (jobs 139375340282 and 139375340219). The two captured lines are the unit fixtures in `e2e/selftest/lib.spec.ts`.
- Count, to compare a later scan with:

| Day | Run / job | Commit | Line | Reported |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-22 | 45070739444 / 137357445578 | `t/T8.F` bbe57b0b | no browser log on that tree | FAILED (unlabelled form) |
| 2026-11-24 | 45356828517 / 138317559276 | `t/T9.50` 2e93d126 | `queued` | FAILED (before the label) |
| 2026-11-25 | 45613593203 / 139204215736 | `m9` 0578360f, the leg alone at repeat 10 | `unqueued` | FAILED (before the label) |

- On `m3` to `m8`, which have no browser log, the fault stays a plain FAILED. Recognise it by hand: the leg's `waitForRequest` times out after 30 s (or `page.reload` rejects with "WebKit encountered an internal error"), the invariants list 16 or 17 "WebKit encountered an internal error" asset loads, and `html data-client-build` is missing. Job 137357445578 is the example.

### T9.55g — WebKit's page process abort in GLib, labelled

- Finding (Playwright 1.61.1 WebKit, WPE build 2311, Linux): in `export.spec.ts:46` and `:67`, the two Save as PDF legs whose new tab keeps its opener, the opener's page process writes `GLib:ERROR:../../../glib/gmain.c:3993:g_main_context_check_unlocked: assertion failed: (i <= 0 || fds[i - 1].fd < fds[i].fd)` to the browser's stderr 65 to 107 ms after the browser creates the tab's view, then aborts. The tab never sends its first request, the leg's 10 s wait for the `page` event gives up, and the engine reports the crash 17.7 to 31.8 s after the abort, as the teardown's evaluate fails with `Target crashed`. Seen with the browser's log in runs 45382272809 (m9), 45382184674 and 45618050448.
- Rate, the number a later scan is compared with: 11 failures in 902 executions of the two legs in every `e2e (webkit, workspace)` job from 2026-11-13 to 11-25 (1.2 % a leg, 2.4 % a job); 1 in 144 on `m9`; 0 in 451 for the `noopener` leg and 0 in 912 in Chromium.
- Reproduction (throwaway selftests): 6 aborts in 256 tries of the journey's own steps (runs 45634000017 and 45634004659), 0 in 242 tries of the same click path in a plain context, 0 in 275 and 469 tries with no product code. Not established: whether the harness's context is needed (the arms ran on different runners, which differ as much as the arms do: 5 aborts in 75 tries on one, 1 in 142 on the other), or what native code breaks GLib's descriptor order. No backtrace exists; `engine.json` now records `ulimit -c` and `/proc/sys/kernel/core_pattern`, which say whether a core could be written.
- The label: `Actors.settle` reports a failed WebKit test BLOCKED with the fixed name when a GLib line was noted after its `Actors` was made and before its teardown began, and one of its people's pages reported a crash at or after that line. The message names the person, the line's time and the seconds to the crash report, with the teardown's own error beneath it in the thrown error only; the summary prints only the fixed name. A page crash without the line, the line without a crash, a line from an earlier test, a crash before the line and an export that fails with its page alive stay FAILED. `settle({ assert: false })` runs no evaluate on the dead page and cannot label. What BLOCKED means and the two-rows rule are as for T9.55c (docs/METHOD.md); the product's part in the trigger is not excluded, so a change that made the abort common must be read, not run again.
- Selftests red in [run 45667517552](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45667517552) (jobs 139394716623 and 139394716625: a killed page process gave Playwright's `crash` event and the teardown failed with `page.evaluate: Target crashed`, FAILED), green in [run 45668716608](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/45668716608) (jobs 139398966899 and 139398966945).
- Against the real fault: the reproduction, reshaped so each try ends in `Actors.settle` on its body's outcome, ran twice on the task branch (runs 45670116317 and 45670121155, jobs 139404043035 and 139404300851): 175 and 181 tries, no abort, so no real abort was labelled or missed. Its only two failed tries were a setup request's `socket hang up`, FAILED and not labelled.
- A newer engine (open question 4): with `@playwright/test` and the image at 1.65.1 (WebKit build 2388) the kept reproduction ran twice at repeat 2 (runs 45667785818 and 45667790094): 202 tries (jobs 139395658855: 123 in fresh browsers, 79 in the long-lived one) and 170 tries (job 139395709332: 103 and 67), no abort in 372. The same afternoon the reshaped reproduction on 1.61.1 also had none in 356, so this does not show the newer build is free of the fault. The suite's files loaded on 1.65.1 (the reproduction runs on the selftest fixture, which imports `e2e/lib/engine.ts`); the rest of the suite was not run on it. Nothing of that branch is kept.
- On `m3` to `m8`, which have no browser log or crash record, the fault stays a plain FAILED. Recognise it by hand: the `page` timeout at `export.spec.ts:55` or `:89`, followed by `Target crashed` in the teardown (`e2e/lib/invariants.ts:253` or `:270`).
- Real Safari, for the owner's hand check (open question 5): the suite never exercises the pop-up blocker (the product opens the tab 1.1 to 1.5 s after the click on a CI runner, after two animation frames and several awaits), the print dialog (every leg replaces `window.print`) or the session hand-off to the new tab in Safari. Once before the pilot: choose Save as PDF on a note, and on a shared note as a link-only reader; a new tab and the print dialog should appear, and "Failed to open PDF preview" would mean the blocker refused it.

## T9.56 on m9: the full test run fails only on a real regression, part 5

### T9.56g — the slash-table helper types its last letter after the menu shows Table

- What was wrong: the parity job's "the slash menu inserts a table" (`e2e/parity/first-click.spec.ts`, helper `slashTable` in `e2e/lib/first-click.ts`) failed 8 of 187 parity jobs that ran it from 2026-11-13 to 11-27 (4.3 %, every one on the "existing note" legs), not "seen once". Pristine Moss at the pin binds the slash menu's keys in a passive effect whose Enter handler closes over the list of that render, so an Enter that lands between the menu's repaint and that effect runs the previous list: an empty one inserts a plain new line, a Tabs-first one inserts a Tabs block. The helper typed "/table", polled until the first row read Table and pressed Enter at once, and nothing in the page changes when the keys are rebound, so the poll could not see the weak step.
- What changed: the helper types "/tabl", waits for Table first, types "e" and presses Enter. A letter rebinds the keys in its own task, and once the poll has passed every list that can be bound at Enter (unfiltered, "tabl", "table") has Table first. No longer wait, no retry, no wider bound. The change ships no test: no deterministic red was reached, so only the helper's comment guards it against a later edit that presses Enter straight after a poll again.
- Evidence. The delay arm, rebuilt from the saved harness (every MessageChannel message held 300 ms), in one parity job ([run 46031702732](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031702732), job 140627246381): the helper as it was failed 18 of 40 (12 of 20 instrumented, every one with a stale Enter handler; 4 of 10 bare; 2 of 10 on the new note); the fixed helper failed 0 of 40. The parity lane on the change, five times green: runs [46031794004](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031794004), [46031803429](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031803429), [46031811813](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031811813), [46031820713](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031820713), [46031829119](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031829119); the pinned cells are as before in all five (new note, quick click [2,2]; the other three [1,0]). At 4.3 % a job, five green jobs say only that the change did no harm. `j01-editing` at repeat 3 in both engines green ([run 46031837974](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031837974); its first attempt lost one WebKit leg, `j01 registers: open HTML drafts…`, to WebKit's "Network process crashed", the known engine fault, and the re-run of the failed job was green); the product twin (`j01-editing.spec.ts:527`) 10 of 10 in each engine ([run 46031853428](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031853428)).
- These runs are the Moss half of deviation 28: on the pin's own build, "/table" and Enter in the state the deviation guards against inserted a Tabs block or a plain new line, where this product inserted the table every time.
- For the Moss team: at the pin, `useTypeahead` binds Enter in a passive effect, so an Enter just after the menu repaints runs the previous list: none, or its first row.
- Not known: why the keys are sometimes rebound 10 to 20 ms after the repaint; why only the "existing note" legs failed; how often a person meets it in Moss's desktop app. `e2e/lib/undo-steps.ts` was left alone: its last key before Enter is a letter and Quote is first for every prefix of "quote" (inferred, not measured).

### T9.56f — every Playwright project's test requests use a connection of their own

- What was wrong: the parity job failed 2 of 148 times in setup with `apiRequestContext.post: socket hang up` (`e2e/parity/parity.spec.ts`, a comment target's `POST /api/docs`). The parity specs take `test` from `@playwright/test` itself, so `ownConnections()`, which T9.50g called only from the journey and selftest fixtures, never ran in their worker, and their Node-side requests rode pooled keep-alive sockets. The local Worker closes a connection 5 s after its last answer and nothing resends, so a request written to a pooled socket as it closed failed.
- What changed: `e2e/playwright.config.ts` calls `ownConnections()` at module scope, which every worker of every project loads before its first request; the two fixture calls and their imports went, and the helper's comment names the one home. The T9.50g line above that says the helper is "called at module scope by both fixtures" is no longer true. Nothing is resent and no timeout changes.
- Evidence. A new parity spec, `e2e/parity/connections.spec.ts`, answers four requests (two through `request.newContext()`, one through `context.request`, one through `page.request`) the way the local Worker does and requires four connections. Red on the commit of the test alone ([run 46031930849](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46031930849), job 140634262423: `Expected: 4`, `Received: 1`, the only failure of 37); green with the config call ([run 46032026462](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46032026462)). T9.50g's selftest (`selftest/lib.spec.ts:147`) green in both engines ([run 46032031113](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46032031113)), and with the slow selftest `selftest/stack.spec.ts:53` (requests handed to the stack as its pooled connections idle out) green in both ([run 46038648585](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46038648585)). The checks lane green, `scripts/deploy/deploy.test.mjs` included ([run 46032035878](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46032035878)).
- Still open: `m3` to `m8` keep the fault until `m9` merges (their parity jobs: 0 of 21, which cannot bound a rate near 1 in 100). The exact gap before either failed request, and how wide the window is on a busy runner, are not known.

### T9.56i — Back or Forward to a note opened by a share link draws no sidebar row

- What was wrong: a person who opened someone's note by its share link, clicked one of their own notes and pressed the browser's Back (or Forward) saw the linked note as an extra, selected row under their own notes, for the listing read's round trip plus about 0.16 s, and until their next visit elsewhere when that read failed. The browser's return goes to Moss's open-note handler, which adds every note it opens to Moss's list (`applyNoteRecordToStore`); the sidebar draws a row for every id in that list, and only the next full listing read took the linked note out again. T9.54h's write is made once before Moss mounts, and nothing watched the list afterwards.
- What changed: the bridge keeps every note the tab learned from its address and not from a listing (`offList`, filled where `admitted` is, never emptied) and answers `outsideListing(id)` on its workspace object: the id is in that set and the listing read last does not carry it. A small guard (`apps/web/src/host/unlisted-notes.ts`), started from `bootMoss` after error analytics install and awaited before the shell is returned, subscribes to Moss's list on Jotai's default store and writes the list again without such a note in the same store write, so no render sees it. A note a listing carries and a note the person has just made are left alone. No word on screen, no vendored file, no server file.
- The walk first (two vaults, a reload, then Back; throwaway runs [46032273960](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46032273960) unchanged and [46032313107](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46032313107) with the guard, 3 per engine each): a person with two vaults opens a note of his own in vault A, goes to a note in vault B, reloads there and presses Back. Unchanged, his vault A note's row showed among vault B's rows 28 to 54 ms after the popstate, and the list swapped to vault A at 208 to 271 ms. With the guard his note's row was withheld until that listing swapped (209 to 233 ms), then shown, in every execution in both engines. So his own note has its row once that listing has answered; if that listing read fails it stays without one until a later re-read (inferred, not run).
- Evidence. Two legs in `j08-link-notes.spec.ts`: Moss's arrow, Back, Forward and Back after a reload on a note of his own, with the row recorder judged at each return, before each forget and after the last typing step; and a Back whose listing read is refused once (429). On the commit of the legs alone ([run 46032565214](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46032565214), jobs 140634770090 and 140634770121) both were red 3 of 3 in both engines, the first at "Forward", "Back" and "Back after a reload on his own note" and never at "Moss's arrow". The first green dispatch at repeat 10 ([run 46036726111](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46036726111)) found no row in any execution, but the refused-read leg failed 1 of 10 in Chromium and 3 of 10 in WebKit on invariant 1: its refusal had taken Moss's own re-read of the list, still pending from the visit just before, and Moss logged its failed hydrate. The leg now refuses only the read that names the note returned to (red without the fix on a throwaway branch of the legs alone, [run 46040846175](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46040846175)); that alone still failed once in WebKit the same way ([run 46040838683](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46040838683)), because Moss's re-read, 150 ms after it hears of a listing read, joins the read in flight. The leg then waits for the visit's own listing read and a page timer of twice Moss's delay before it presses Back: green 10 of 10 in each engine, both legs ([run 46042347613](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46042347613)). That a refused listing read surfaces as Moss's failed-hydrate console error whenever Moss's re-read is pending is the product's behaviour, unchanged here. Unit tests: `outsideListing` (an admitted note, the same note once a listing carries it, a note opened by `byId` after its backlinks read's 404 empties the admitted set) and the guard against a plain Jotai store. The T9.54, T9.19, T9.53, T9.46 and j13 legs at repeat 3 in both engines ([run 46036729800](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46036729800)); the checks lane ([run 46036733424](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46036733424)).
- Not in this change: a note the tab learned from a listing that no longer carries it is not guarded (Back from vault B to a vault A note without a reload still draws its row among vault B's rows until the listing swaps; the same for a note under a folder link after a switch to one's own vault, and a grant revoked while the workspace socket was down). With the guard, a note of the person's own that the tab learned by its address has its row withheld until the listing swaps. The guard also closes a gap of about 24 ms in which a full re-read could remove the record of a note not yet active (inferred, not run). The bell's notices use the same Moss handler; no case is known for a link holder. A vault switch, a split pane and Trash with a linked note open were not run. What the lingering row's menu did (Trash, Move, Pin) was never read or clicked; a real network was not tried.

### T9.56k — the Back-and-Forward legs give the extra socket open to the note the window opened by itself

- What was wrong: `j06-folders.spec.ts`'s "Back through two notes trashed since she left them…" failed once in WebKit in teardown ("invariant 3: 2 socket opens in one document, 1 allowed"; 1 in 206 plain WebKit executions). The leg creates three notes over the API with its window open, then reloads, and allowed one extra socket open for the third note only. When the third create is slow the window has already shown and remembered the second note (T9.48a), reopens it by itself after the reload, and the leg opens it once more. A person meets nothing wrong.
- What changed, test only: both legs read the one pane the reloaded window shows by itself, wait for it to be live and give it the extra open. The sibling "the browser's Back and Forward move between the notes she opened…" now counts A 1, B 2, C 1, plus 1 for that note. The leg "Forward to a note trashed since she left it…" was left alone: it is not exposed by the diagnosis's reading, and it was not probed.
- Evidence. Hold copies of both legs (the third create held until the window has remembered a note), on throwaway branches: unchanged, red 10 of 10 per leg in each engine ([run 46033226014](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46033226014), jobs 140629916207 and 140629916223), the sibling with "4 socket opens, 3 allowed", as inferred, and the other with "2 socket opens, 1 allowed"; changed, green 10 of 10 per leg in each engine ([run 46033261748](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46033261748)). The two legs alone, three dispatches at repeat 10 in both engines, 30 of 30 each ([46038270515](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46038270515), [46038280189](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46038280189), [46038289800](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46038289800)); the T9.19, T9.46 and T9.48a legs at repeat 3 in both engines, no failure ([run 46038298362](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46038298362)). No Back or Forward opened a socket in any of them.
- Not known: the leg's true rate; it depends on how often a create stalls.

### T9.56: questions for the owner

- Deviation 28, still pending: keep Enter in the slash menu choosing the first match for the text actually typed? Moss at the pin is now seen, on its own build, inserting a Tabs block or a plain new line from "/table" and Enter in the state the deviation guards against, and this product inserted the table every time. Recommended: yes, approve it.
- Question 20, still open: should a note opened by a share link have a lasting row in the opener's sidebar? What a person saw before T9.56i was wrong under either answer; T9.56i builds "no row". Recommended: stay with "no row". If the answer is "a lasting row", the guard and T9.54h's boot write give way to a write of the record into the list in the bridge, the T9.54 legs change their assertion, and where the row sits, its menu, what happens to it on revocation and that it exists in one tab only are still to settle.

### T9.56j — a refused Suggest window that holds unsaved text says why and offers Retry (the owner decides before it merges)

- What was wrong: a Suggest window that held the unsaved-text card ("This suggestion was closed while you typed, so your last changes were not saved.") and was then refused its lease, because the person's other windows of the note held all four, was read-only and showed only the card: no sentence about the other windows and no Retry anywhere, so the only way forward on screen was Dismiss, which discards the kept text without asking. The refused window's notice (`suggest-busy`) has no terminal reason, so `NoticeStack` classed it with the outages, the card leads over an outage, and the hidden notice and its Retry were not drawn. T9.41 wrote the ranking when no outage notice had an action; T5.S16 added the first that has one. It arose in every execution that built the state (16 of 16 in the diagnosis).
- What changed: the class of the connection's notice is one pure function beside `arrangeNotices` (`classifyConnectionNotice` in `notice-stack.ts`), and it ranks the refused window's notice with the notices that close the note until the person acts, as the connection limit's is. While it holds kept text the window now shows one card, "This note is open for suggesting in other windows. Close one to suggest here." with "Recover unsaved text (1)" and "Retry"; one press on Recover shows the unsaved-text card above it; after a Retry that succeeds the card leads again and still holds the whole text. No new word, colour or icon; the leave-site guard, both stores, the four-lease limit, the cooldown and the DOM value `suggest-busy` are untouched. The T9.41 order sentence in BUILDPLAN and the `terminal` input's description say so.
- What the person stops seeing, the trade for the owner: while the refused notice leads, the sentence "your last changes were not saved", the card's warning icon and its alert are one press away; the only sign of the kept text is "Recover unsaved text (1)" on a quiet notice.
- Evidence. The leg `j16-suggest-drop busy-with-card suggester…` (the file's twelfth): Ben types, drops, types while down, the owner rejects, two more of his windows take two leases each, he comes back. It requires the notice with its sentence and Retry while the text is undismissed, a read-only note, one refusal, the text whole through Recover then Show text, a reload that asks once, a Retry with both other windows still open that is refused again with the notice, Retry and the whole text back, and, with another window closed, a Retry that makes the window live, the card leading again with the whole text, two refusals in all and no 4429. On the commit of the leg alone: red 3 of 3 in each engine at the first assertion, each with exactly one `lease-cap` refusal in its summary ([run 46043684102](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46043684102)). With the change: 10 of 10 in each engine ([run 46043736782](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46043736782)); the `j16-suggest-drop` and `j16-suggest-unsaved` legs with slow legs at repeat 3 in both engines, `three-tabs` unchanged among them ([run 46043740081](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46043740081)); the checks lane, 'Shard budget' included ([run 46043743395](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46043743395)). Labelled before and after screenshots of the leg's states in both engines, at 1440 wide light and dark and at 390 wide, where the two actions wrap below the sentence inside the card (throwaway runs [46043848288](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46043848288) before and [46043828414](https://github.com/brsbl/moss-multi/actions/runs/46043828414) after, artifacts `e2e-<engine>-all-1`).
- The two pairs the change also touches: a window that still shows an undismissed "no room for more suggestions" notice and is then refused now leads with the refused notice, and the no-room sentence is not drawn until Retry succeeds (inferred, not run); while Moss's Trash notice shows, a terminal lead is dropped, so a refused-window notice there is not drawn where before it was drawn as an outage (no live route to that state was found; not run).
- Not covered by the leg: the editor role, two payloads ("Recover unsaved text (2)"), the other order of arrival (refused first, then text offered back, where the card's alert is never mounted), "Leave" on the browser's question, and how often a person has one note open for suggesting in three windows. A Retry pressed with the other windows open is a second counted refusal; one more in the same minute would start the 60 s cooldown on all of the person's windows of the note, and nothing on the notice says so (inferred, not run). The app menu still reads "Connected / All changes saved" and the row under the notices still says "Suggesting" while the window is refused.

### T9.56j: questions for the owner

- When a window both holds kept text and is refused, which leads? The reason with Retry and "Recover unsaved text (1)" on it, as T9.56j builds; or the card, with the refused-window notice drawn as a second card under it. Recommended: the first; it is what the connection limit and the cooldown already do, and two cards at once is what the one-lead design set out to avoid. The cost is that the warning sentence and its alert are one press away while the window is refused.
- Question 18, still open: should Dismiss on the unsaved-text card ask, or be undoable? It discards text that was never copied with no question, and until T9.56j merges it is the only way to Retry in this state. Recommended: yes, make it undoable; it needs a word a person sees, so it is not built here.
- While the card leads, should a lost connection and an available update still be said in the note? The card hides both sentences. Recommended: no change; neither hides an action without another route (the lost-connection notice has none, and Reload is in the app menu).

### From T8.F's staging runs (2026-11-22 to 23)

**Where the launch check stands.** It closes on commit `7b55f7a`, the build that passed on staging: deploy 45135817095 ran the whole suite in one attempt (823 legs, 1 recovered drop whose reloaded copy matched the server), the security sweep passed 52 of 52, and its full lane 45132454263 is green. The branch head is that build plus documents. A later client change (the app asks again at once after a lost sync message) moved to its own task, T8.G, on branch `t/T8.G`; its two staging runs failed broadly on an afternoon when the CI runners' requests reached the platform by an unusual route, so they show nothing about it either way, and a change to sync code does not ship on reasoning alone.

Recorded limits (accepted, not open findings):

- After a lost sync message whose acknowledgement is also lost, a second person's code, HTML or formula block catches up 10 to 14 s late. Nothing is lost. The j01-registers black-hole check keeps the 40 s bound this build ran with until T8.G restores 10 s.
- The connection-drop gate compares text only, and counts a closed pane as recovered.
- Re-running only the failed jobs of a suite shard is a named human decision and is not gated by earlier verdicts.
- The demo screenshots are from the first demo run; nothing visible changed since.
- The j15 comment-composer leg repeats its gesture until the composer opens.

Open:

- Staging is at its growth cap: 29,360 note objects against 30,000, and a full suite needs 1,200. No further full-suite deploy can start until staging is pruned or the cap is changed.
- On 2026-11-23, between about 14:00 and 22:00 UTC, requests from the CI runners entered the platform through unusual sites. Full-suite runs in that window (45189582791, 45245524556) are not evidence about the product.
- 13 Worker exceptions within one minute (21:40 UTC, deploy 45245524556: HTTP 500 on comments, access, backlinks and content) have no known cause.
- Whether a suggester's text typed around a connection drop survives the 60 s suggestion cooldown (close code 4429) is being checked separately.

Deploy 45135817095 of `7b55f7a` passed the full suite in one attempt, both engines, with no leg re-run, 1 recovered drop (export `same`) and no infrastructure retry; the sweep on that build passed 52 of 52. Each earlier failure below is fixed at its cause: the setup resend (`setupFetch`), the review retry after a Durable Object reset (`retryingReset`, `retriedReview`), and the leave-aware chunk logging and recovery (WebKit stops a page's loads as a navigation starts; red in rehearsal 45121810915, 6 of 6, green in 45132450810, 12 of 12). The j16-paste leg past the cap passed 6 of 6 in that rehearsal and in the deploy; the staging log now names a failure with no kind by its error class.

Failures in the two full-suite deploys before those fixes, per leg:

| Deploy | Leg | Engine | What failed | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 45083542524 | j16-suggest routed deletes: past an inline link | Chromium | an undeclared try-again close (1013) about 6 s after open | D1 access check past its deadline (staging timing) |
| 45083542524 | j16-suggest Edit-mode marks: a code block | WebKit | a setup request failed before the second principal existed | test setup on a closed keep-alive connection (fixed by `setupFetch`) |
| 45083542524 | j16-census-blocks: heading before the boundary | WebKit | HTTP 500 on suggestion accept, as the doc's Durable Object reset (both peers dropped 1006 at once) | product: a DocDO reset mid-accept answers 500 |
| 45083542524 | run total | both | 9 recovered drops, 2 allowed; 6 were three DocDO resets during j16-census-blocks legs | platform resets |
| 45101189479 | j01-registers: three legs (warm-stack code block, read-only code field, formula popover move) | Chromium | a setup request failed (`kind request`) | test setup on a closed keep-alive connection (fixed by `setupFetch`) |
| 45101189479 | j16-census-edges: leading heading after the boundary | WebKit | a page error and console error on Ben's page | product, undiagnosed (text withheld off loopback) |
| 45101189479 | j16-paste: a large paste on an open suggestion past the cap | WebKit | an assertion of an unnamed kind | undiagnosed |
